\section*{Scope and interpretation}
This appendix is an engineering and legal-triage rendering of the declarative DFM9 source-rights dependency directed acyclic graph (DAG); it is not legal advice. It contains 161 effective training datasets, 286 dependency rows, 556 typed edges, and 22 manual decisions. Each top-level source cites concrete dependency rows by identifier. Dependencies may themselves cite further dependency rows, and therefore shared components are stated once rather than duplicated under every parent.

The status fields distinguish the node's local decision from the computed DAG status. A computed \texttt{cleared} status means that the node's own layer and all required dependencies are cleared for the stated scientific-research use. Lineage completeness is independent: \texttt{top\_level\_only} indicates that the current working decision exists but further provenance decomposition remains desirable. A dagger ($\dagger$) marks an informational edge that is not required for upward clearance.

\section{Copyright and data-protection audit framework}
\subsection{Two independent legal gates}
The project treats copyright and database rights on the one hand, and data protection on the other, as independent gates. A copyright licence or text-and-data-mining (TDM) exception does not supply a GDPR legal basis, and a GDPR legal basis does not authorise acts restricted by copyright or database rights. Passing one review therefore never cures a failure in the other. The operational starting point is to identify the exact effective source, revision, acquisition route, retained fields, transformations, repetitions, and upstream dependencies before assigning either status.
\subsection{Copyright and TDM decision path}
The first preference is a direct basis: public-domain status, an applicable open licence, assigned rights, express permission, or an institutional agreement. Terms are applied component by component where a mixture or derivative has multiple rights layers. Attribution, notice, share-alike, non-commercial, field-of-use, and redistribution conditions remain attached to the relevant layer. Synthetic or transformed data are not presumed rights-free merely because a model or script made them; retained source expression and seed provenance remain dependencies.
Where no sufficient direct basis covers a reproduction or extraction, the project considers Articles 3 and 4 of Directive (EU) 2019/790 (the DSM Directive), as implemented in Denmark by Copyright Act sections 11 c and 11 b.\footnote{DSM Directive: \url{https://eur-lex.europa.eu/eli/dir/2019/790/oj}. Danish Copyright Act: \url{https://www.retsinformation.dk/eli/lta/2023/1093}.} Article 3 is the research route: it covers reproductions and extractions by a qualifying research organisation or cultural-heritage institution for scientific research, only for material to which it has lawful access. Copies must be secured and may be retained for research and verification; contractual terms may not override that exception. The project records Article 3 as a purpose- and actor-specific basis, not as a general licence or permission to redistribute source material.
Article 4 is the general TDM route. It likewise requires lawful access, permits retention only for as long as necessary for TDM, and is unavailable where the rightsholder expressly reserved the use in an appropriate manner, including machine-readable means for online content. The project therefore records acquisition-time terms, repository cards, robots or TDM signals, and other reservation evidence where available. Missing evidence is not converted into an assumption that no reservation existed. Article 4 determinations in this register are source-specific and must be rechecked for a fresh acquisition or a materially different use. Neither TDM article authorises publication of protected training copies or guarantees that generated outputs are non-infringing.
\subsection{GDPR interpretation and controls}
For sources that contain or may contain personal data, the project separately records the controller, purposes, categories and sources of data, Article 6 basis, any Article 9 condition, transparency route, retention, access controls, data-subject-rights handling, and whether a data-protection impact assessment is required. Public availability does not by itself remove GDPR protection or make the processing reasonably expected. Data minimisation, purpose limitation, accuracy, storage limitation, security, and accountability apply throughout collection, preparation, training, evaluation, release, and deployment.
The project reads EDPB Opinion 28/2024 as requiring a documented, case-by-case assessment rather than a categorical claim that trained models are anonymous.\footnote{EDPB Opinion 28/2024: \url{https://www.edpb.europa.eu/documents/opinion-of-the-board-art-64/opinion-282024-on-certain-data-protection-aspects-related-to_en}.} Where legitimate interest is relied upon, the record must identify a lawful, precise, real and present interest; establish necessity and the absence of a less intrusive effective alternative; and balance that interest against the rights and reasonable expectations of affected people, with concrete mitigations. Unlawful upstream processing may affect later deployment, especially where personal data remain in the model.
The EDPB's 2026 Guidelines 1/2026 on scientific research, 02/2026 on anonymisation, and 03/2026 on web scraping for generative AI are treated as consultation drafts, not final law or independent legal bases.\footnote{EDPB 2026 consultations: \url{https://www.edpb.europa.eu/public-consultations_en}.} They are nevertheless used as conservative operational guidance: define the research purpose and safeguards specifically; assess anonymisation against record isolation, linkage, and inference using means reasonably likely for the relevant actors; and, for web-derived data, document provenance and collection time, source reliability, minimisation, accuracy, transparency, and incidental special-category data. Their draft status and later revisions must be checked at release and on material reuse.
\subsection{EU AI Act scope and open-source release strategy}
The EU AI Act is a third, independent compliance layer.\footnote{Regulation (EU) 2024/1689: \url{https://eur-lex.europa.eu/eli/reg/2024/1689/oj}. Commission GPAI scope guidelines: \url{https://digital-strategy.ec.europa.eu/en/library/guidelines-scope-obligations-providers-general-purpose-ai-models-under-ai-act}.} It does not replace copyright, GDPR, contractual, research-ethics, or product-sector review. Article 2(6) excludes models specifically developed and put into service for the sole purpose of scientific research and development; Article 2(8) separately excludes research, testing, and development before market placement or putting into service. The project does not choose Article 2(6) as its principal release position. The intended public release permits broad inspection, reuse, modification, redistribution, and downstream integration rather than confining the model and every derivative to a sole scientific-R\&D purpose. Article 2(8) also ceases to answer the question once the model is released. Relying on either exclusion would therefore make the legal position depend on a narrow purpose limitation that conflicts with the chosen release objective.
Instead, DFM-Mimir is published under the Apache License 2.0 on the Danish Foundation Models Hugging Face repository, with its weights, architecture/configuration, chat template, and model-usage instructions publicly available.\footnote{DFM-Mimir release: \url{https://huggingface.co/danish-foundation-models/DFM-Mimir}.} Apache-2.0 permits access, use, modification, and distribution without a non-commercial or research-only purpose restriction and therefore supports the Article 53(2) free/open-source conditions. This verified release fact supersedes the older scope-dossier assumption that MIMIR License v1.0, a non-commercial/research-only licence, governed the release. The model licence grants only rights controlled by the provider: it does not relicense third-party training works, remove their attribution or non-commercial conditions, or authorise infringing model outputs.
Open source is chosen for substantive and regulatory reasons. It supports independent inspection, reproducibility, adaptation, Danish-language research and innovation, security review, and broad downstream access without forcing the project to police a sole-research purpose. If the model is nevertheless classified as a general-purpose AI (GPAI) model, Article 53(2) exempts a qualifying non-systemic open-source provider from Article 53(1)(a)--(b): the authority-facing technical-documentation duty and the downstream-provider information duty. It does not exempt the Article 53(1)(c) Union-copyright compliance policy or the Article 53(1)(d) sufficiently detailed public training-content summary, and the exemption does not apply to a GPAI model with systemic risk. Open source likewise does not exempt downstream AI systems from their own applicable obligations. This dossier retains fuller documentation voluntarily because it supports accountability and makes the project robust if classification or guidance changes.
\subsection{Training-compute thresholds}
The Commission's current, non-binding GPAI scope guidelines use a conjunctive indicative criterion of more than $10^{23}$ cumulative training FLOPs and generation of language, text-to-image, or text-to-video. The statutory definition remains capability-based: a model below the indicative compute value can still be GPAI if it displays significant generality and competently performs a wide range of distinct tasks, while an exceptional model above it may lack such generality. Article 51(2) separately presumes high-impact capabilities, and therefore systemic-risk GPAI status, above $10^{25}$ cumulative training FLOPs; the Commission may also designate a model on equivalent capabilities or impact under Article 51(1).
The project's recurrence-aware engineering upper bound for the released DFM9/Mimir training run is $1.19\times10^{22}$ FLOPs. It counts multiply-add as two operations, assumes five backpropagation steps from the beginning, and covers the documented 1,650,000-step lifecycle. The estimate is 11.9\% of $10^{23}$ (about 8.4 times below the indicative GPAI compute criterion) and 0.119\% of $10^{25}$ (about 840 times below the systemic-risk compute presumption). Thus compute does not trigger either current threshold, even under the project's conservative recurrence assumption. The value must still receive independent technical review, remain bound to the exact checkpoint and methodology, and be updated for continued training or other development in the same model lifecycle. The below-threshold result supports, but does not alone decide, the GPAI or systemic-risk classification.
\subsection{Audit workflow and role of memorisation}
\begin{enumerate}
\item Freeze a source inventory and exposure manifest, including revisions, files, fields, transformations, row counts, token exposure, repetition, and acquisition evidence.
\item Decompose mixtures and derivatives into a dependency DAG; evaluate every required rights layer and preserve notices and obligations transitively.
\item Apply direct permissions first, then a documented Article 3 or source-specific Article 4 analysis where necessary. Exclude, replace, or escalate unresolved material.
\item Run the independent GDPR review, including personal- and special-category-data screening, legal basis, necessity and balancing, transparency, rights, security, retention, and DPIA/DPO escalation.
\item Audit outputs and model behaviour for extractability and memorisation, record residual risk, and make release and access controls proportionate to the evidence.
\item Reassess when a source, reservation signal, purpose, controller, training exposure, model, attack method, or release mode materially changes.
\end{enumerate}
Memorisation matters to both gates. Verbatim or identifying extraction can increase copyright risk in outputs and can show that personal data remain practically extractable or associated with training subjects, weakening an anonymity claim. Conversely, a negative finite audit cannot prove non-infringement, erase an unlawful collection, establish anonymity, or rule out stronger adaptive attacks. It is one empirical control within the broader provenance, legal-basis, minimisation, security, and release assessment.
\section{DFM9 memorisation audit and results}
The Mimir technical report describes two independent memorisation audits over four rights/provenance categories (A--D).\footnote{Mimir v1 technical report, Appendix C: \url{https://arxiv.org/html/2608.13517}.} Category A contains synthetic instruction data seeded from agreement-covered sources; B contains Hugging Face instruction data for which row-level opt-out status could not be established reliably; C contains Hugging Face instruction data with high confidence that no applicable row-level opt-out had been exercised; and D contains other synthetic and reasoning data with no identified material copyright concern but without a coherent open-licence, licensed, or public-domain status.
\subsection{Prefix and generic-prompt audit}
The first audit used 1,000 ordinary non-adversarial prompts across Danish and English and 500 targeted 50-token-prefix extraction prompts per category, 3,000 prompts in total, against 140,990,504 training documents. In generic settings, matches of at least 50 tokens appeared only once for English/B and once for English/D, and not in the other English or any Danish comparison. Under prefix attacks, any-length matches were found in 0.073\% of A documents, 0.887\% of B, 0.029\% of C, and 0.046\% of D. Spans of at least 50 tokens occurred in 26, 271, 293, and 257 documents respectively: 0.00022\%, 0.015\%, 0.00031\%, and 0.00074\% of the checked documents. Average longest spans were 8.8, 17.1, 12.4, and 22.3 tokens. Longer B and D matches were often numbers, formulae, matrices, code, algorithms, structured tasks, or mathematical solutions.
\subsection{Exact 64-token continuation and content adjudication}
The second audit used a fixed greedy 64-token prefix plus 64-token continuation protocol. Across 136,612,444 model-input evaluations it found 5,562 exact-match occurrences (0.0041\%), representing 3,423 unique prefix--continuation pairs. The category counts were 15/2,732,080 for A (0.0005\%), 7/411,508 for B (0.0017\%), 4,874/124,246,748 for C (0.0039\%), and 666/9,222,108 for D (0.0072\%). LLM adjudication followed by human review classified 61/5,562 matches (1.10\%) as coherent prose and one (0.018\%) as expressive prose. The report identified no high-priority copyright finding; its single medium-priority case was a predictable continuation of a traditional repetitive song.
\subsection{Conclusion and limits}
The combined evidence did not show systematic reconstruction of long, distinctive expressive passages and supports a low observed copyright-related memorisation risk under the tested conditions. Potentially risky outputs represented 62 of 136,612,444 model-input evaluations (approximately 0.000045\%). This is a measured result, not a zero-risk finding: adaptive prompts, longer prefixes, alternative decoding, future attacks, and personal-data-specific extraction or inference may produce different results. The audit therefore supports, but does not by itself establish, GDPR anonymity or copyright clearance.

\section{Manual decisions and overrides}
This register states every project-owner decision cited by the DAG. References in the preceding tables include both decisions stated at a node and decisions inherited through its dependencies. The decisions are purpose-limited and do not replace the residual qualifications or future testing targets stated here.

\manid{MAN-001} & `allenai/Dolci-Instruct-SFT-Tool-Use`: SimFC unmatched/adapted API schemas & Accepted as low risk. Most schemas match xLAM or ToolACE; the unmatched 92,800-row upper bound may include adapted public MCP APIs. No material reason to invoke Article 3 was identified. & Exact API provenance is incomplete; preserve notices where known. & Probe uncommon API names, descriptions, argument schemas, and exact schema continuations in the unmatched subset. \\
\manid{MAN-002} & Same DOLCI Tool Use family: Semantic Scholar titles, abstracts, and occasional paper content in M3/M4v2/M5v2 & Accepted as low risk. The generated trajectory layer and non-commercial Semantic Scholar API use are direct; retained paper content has heterogeneous terms. No material reason to invoke Article 3 was identified. & Per-paper licence coverage is incomplete. & Prefix extraction against retained abstracts/titles, stratified by recorded paper licence and missing-licence rows. \\
\manid{MAN-003} & Same DOLCI Tool Use family: DRv4 Serper search-result snippets & Accepted as low risk. Fetched pages were summarized, while short search snippets remain in environment messages. No material reason to invoke Article 3 was identified. & Source-site terms and snippet provenance are incomplete. & Exact and approximate extraction from distinctive retained snippets. \\
\manid{MAN-004} & Same DOLCI Tool Use family: five residual OpenScholar-labelled rows/four unique prompts & Accepted as low risk because the residual set is extremely small and the generated trajectory layer is direct. No material reason to invoke Article 3 was identified. & Prompt release was not located. & Exhaustive prompt/response extraction test for all four unique prompts. \\
\manid{MAN-005} & `allenai/verifiable-reasoning-filtered-gpt-41` and `allenai/verifiable-reasoning-filtered-o4-mini`: complete RLVE prompt family & Accepted for current research after a 250-variant prompt audit. This includes 45 close/constrained, 15 expressive/source-specific, one unavailable, and four unmatched variants; Article 3 remains the fallback where protected expression is retained. & Fifteen variants retain the strongest source-specific expression; four are unmatched and one source was unavailable. & Test all 15 carryover variants, prioritising `blockimage`, `powernest`, `fbi\_binarytree`, and `abprogramsimulation`; also test unavailable/unmatched variants. \\
\manid{MAN-006} & Sapient Sudoku Extreme, especially Enjoy Sudoku/community puzzle collections & Accepted as low risk without Article 3. Individual grids and solutions are functional records; only possible selection/arrangement or database rights in unlicensed community collections remained. & Compilation/database-right evidence remains incomplete. & Puzzle-prefix/solution extraction and membership inference, grouped by named community collection. \\
\manid{MAN-007} & Sapient factual-FLAN RACE & Article 4 / Danish section 11 b chosen based on lawful long-running public research/HF distribution, no identified reservation, and no known rightsholder challenge. & No explicit open content licence; acquisition-time reservation evidence is incomplete. & Prefix extraction from exam passages and exact question/choice reproduction, stratified by source exam. \\
\manid{MAN-008} & Sapient factual-FLAN DREAM & Same Article 4 determination as RACE. & Human-authored examination dialogues have no captured open content licence; acquisition-time evidence is incomplete. & Dialogue-prefix extraction and exact question/choice reproduction. \\
\manid{MAN-009} & Sapient factual-FLAN WebQuestions & Same Article 4 determination; public crowd-authored questions and Freebase answers were long distributed without a known reservation/challenge. & No explicit licence for the crowd-authored question collection was found. & Exact prompt extraction and rare-name/entity completion. \\
\manid{MAN-010} & Sapient factual-FLAN uncovered CoQA portions, principally RACE-derived examination material & Article 4 chosen for layers not already covered by source-specific direct terms. & CoQA is a mixed-source corpus; the decision does not alter CC-BY-SA, MSR-LA, or Apache obligations on covered components. & Test only source-uncovered/RACE-derived rows separately from directly licensed CoQA sources. \\
\manid{MAN-011} & Sampled TriviaQA question/short-answer rows across all 14 question-source groups & Accepted the official repository statement that Apache-2.0 applies to code and data as the operative basis. This supersedes the earlier Article 3/4 split, including JetPunk and TriviaCountry. & UW separately disclaims ownership of included questions/documents; current robots findings remain evidence. The decision excludes evidence documents. & Exact question/answer extraction by `question\_source`, with dedicated JetPunk and TriviaCountry strata. \\
\manid{MAN-012} & WildChat retained human prompts/conversations and descendants including `synquid/ifbench-train` and `synquid/wildchat-100k-qwen-messages` & Accepted affirmative user consent for research/product-development use and third-party publication/sharing as express permission for current research training. & Privacy/GDPR safeguards remain independent; permission does not eliminate personal-data controls. & Canaries, rare-string extraction, and user-prompt memorisation tests, stratified by original versus generated continuation rows and privacy-risk flags. \\
\manid{MAN-013} & `ai2-adapt-dev/flan\_v2\_converted`, including copies inherited by Tulu 3, DOLCI, and Apertus & Article 4 / Danish section 11 b selected for uncovered retained upstream task expression; direct terms continue to govern identified components. This supersedes the initial Article 3 fallback. & The 89,982-row release lacks task-level licence metadata; task provenance and acquisition-time reservation evidence remain partial. & Stratified prefix extraction and membership/propensity testing by recoverable FLAN/OpenOrca task family, prioritising long passages and distinctive questions. \\
\manid{MAN-014} & `allenai/SciRIFF`, including retained rows in Tulu 3, DOLCI, Apertus, and related mixtures & Article 4 / Danish section 11 b selected for uncovered scholarly expression; ODC-By covers the task/schema layer and direct source terms apply where captured. This supersedes the initial Article 3 fallback. & Approximately 39 task families can retain portions of scientific papers; document lineage and acquisition-time reservation evidence remain partial. & Prefix extraction and approximate-match testing against retained paper text, stratified by task family, source licence, and document availability. \\
\manid{MAN-015} & `open-r1/Mixture-of-Thoughts` math, code, science, and generated-trace components & Residual source-expression and editorial risk accepted for the current project based on immediate package/per-sample terms and the recorded prompt-level assessment. No material reason to invoke Article 3 was identified; this is a project-owner risk acceptance, not a blanket upstream relicensing. & Competition/editorial and forum provenance remains incomplete; the `codeforces-cots` card and README use inconsistent CC-BY-4.0/ODC-By labels. & Prioritise AoPS, AMC/AIME, long science questions, complete editorials, and exact/fuzzy trace-to-editorial overlap. \\
\manid{MAN-016} & Uncovered OpenHermes 2.5 components: Airoboros 2.2, Caseus custom, CoT Alpaca GPT-4, and residual Open-Platypus material & Article 4 / Danish section 11 b selected for retained source expression not covered by captured component terms. This supersedes the initial Article 3 fallback; direct terms continue to govern covered components. & Work-level provenance, acquisition-time reservation evidence, and the lost CoT-Alpaca source page remain incomplete. & Stratify extraction testing by `openhermes\_source`, with separate Airoboros, Caseus, CoT-Alpaca, and Platypus cohorts in both repaired English and translated Danish derivatives. \\
\manid{MAN-017} & `zai-org/LongAlign-10k`: all eleven content groups, including the 1,621 full-document copyright-marker rows & Approved on 2026-08-18 for the current academic/non-commercial scientific-research training under Article 3 / Danish section 11 c. The approval expressly includes 760 restrictive, 56 mixed, 640 generic, 89 public-domain-marker, 70 open-licence-marker, and six government-notice rows found by full-document scanning. & This accepts the current research-TDM boundary; it does not establish document-level licences, negate restrictive notices, authorize raw redistribution, or clear Article 4/commercial use. & Exhaustive marker cohort plus stratified samples from the 8,267 no-marker rows; prioritize books/publisher material, long prompts, restrictive notices, late markers, and rare domains. \\
\manid{MAN-018} & `utter-project/EuroBlocks-SFT-Synthetic-1124`: 5,169 source-retaining rows/2,607 unique embedded documents and 134,819 seed-derived rows & Approved on 2026-08-18 for the current academic/non-commercial scientific-research training under Article 3 / Danish section 11 c, with direct terms retained for generated layers. & The annealing corpus and many source URLs/licences remain unidentified; eight unique documents have restrictive notices and 2,474 have no explicit marker. Approval does not authorize raw redistribution or general/non-research use. & Test all 2,607 unique document hashes with occurrence-weighted reporting, plus derived-row overlap tests by language and content group; prioritize restrictive, long, rare-domain, and no-marker documents. \\
\manid{MAN-019} & Sapient retained non-factual FLAN: CoT, FLAN-2021, NIv2, and T0/P3 materializations & Applied the prior MAN-013 Article 4 / Danish section 11 b determination consistently to uncovered FLAN-v2 source expression in the Sapient materialization. Direct source terms remain controlling where identified. & NIv2 explicitly says instance licences follow original datasets; P3 and Google repository licences do not necessarily relicense all source expression. Acquisition-time reservation evidence remains incomplete. & Stratify extraction by the four submixtures and canonical source task; prioritize long passages, unusual instructions, and source families lacking specific terms. \\
\manid{MAN-020} & Sapient Tasksource residual: 84 files / 69,758,538.8 tokens per epoch with blank, `unknown`, `other`, or generic `cc` source-repository metadata & Approved for the current academic/non-commercial scientific-research training under Article 3 / Danish section 11 c. The 77 files with specific recognized licences remain on their direct terms. & Tasksource is a harmonizer rather than a blanket source-rights grant. This decision does not resolve privacy, raw redistribution, nonresearch use, or missing provenance inside derived repositories. & Stratify by upstream repository and metadata class; prioritize CoNLL/Reuters-derived corpora, social/user text, long passages, legal/medical text, and rare labels or formulations. \\
\manid{MAN-021} & All 70 `schneiderkamplab/sapient-synth-*` effective datasets & Manually approved by the project owner on 2026-08-18 for the current academic/non-commercial scientific-research training. The generated and audited replacement datasets are accepted as the operative training works; named upstream-task links remain provenance evidence but no longer block clearance of these synthetic derivatives. & This does not relicense or independently clear the named upstream datasets, authorize redistribution of their source records, or erase privacy and memorisation-testing obligations. & Preserve task-family strata and test exact/fuzzy overlap against named source tasks, prioritising long, distinctive, review, dialogue, and benchmark-derived prompts. \\
\manid{MAN-022} & ShareGPT participant expression retained in `allenai/tulu-v2-sft-mixture`, `allenai/tulu-v2-sft-long-mixture`, and the Tulu-v2 half of `allenai/SciRIFF-train-mix` & The project owner accepts users' deliberate one-click publication to a service designed for public sharing, browsing, and public-API access as participant permission for the current academic/non-commercial research training. This gives the source the same operative cleared status as WildChat for this project. & The evidence is not equivalent to WildChat's explicit research/product-development consent: no ShareGPT term expressly authorizing model training was found. This decision is not a blanket Apache-2.0 licence, raw-redistribution permission, or nonresearch authorization; privacy/GDPR and credential controls remain independent. & Test source-ID-stratified rare strings, credentials/PII flags, and prefix extraction; report the split and Long variants separately and deduplicate by original ShareGPT ID where both are retained. \\
